Message 
The Economic Census provides a count of establishments and employment engaged 
in various economic activities , inclusive of both  organised and un -organised sector. T he 
census helps to bridge the data gaps, provides base frame for follow -up surveys and is also 
useful in development planning. 
 The Sixth Economic Census was conducted by -and-large during October , 2013 to 
April, 2014 by the Directorate of Economics & Statistics in the State. I hope  that the valuable 
data set s presented in this report  will be useful to understand the  structural changes in the 
economy and spur considerable research activities in the State. 
 I appre ciate the efforts taken by Directorate of Economics & Statistics and other 
concerned offices of the State. 
 
 
(Sunil Porwal) 
\n--- PDF PAGE 5 ---\nPreface 
 The Central Statistical Office (CSO),  GoI conducted Sixth Economic Census (Sixth 
EC) in 2013 in collaboration with the Directorate of Econom ics and Statistics in the State.   
All establishments engaged  in various agricultural  (excluding crop production & plantation) 
and non -agricultural  (excluding public administration, defen ce and compulsory social 
securities) activities were covered in the Sixth EC.  The fieldwork of Sixth EC was conducted 
in Maharashtra  by-and-large during  October, 2013 to April, 2014 .  This report ‘Sixth 
Economic Census - Maharashtra State’, provides data on various aspects of the economic 
activities in the State. 
2. I am thankful for  encouragement and guidance provided by the CSO, GoI and 
Honorable Chief Secretary & Additional Chief Secretary (Planning), GoM. I am also thankful 
to various committees constituted for the Sixth EC, concerned officers and all employees of 
DES, Maharashtra for their co-operation.   
 
 
 
          (P. D. Sohale) 
Mumbai                 Director of Economics & Statistics, 
October, 2016                    Government of Maharashtra.

\n--- PDF PAGE 7 ---\nHIGHLIGHTS OF THE SIXTH ECONOMIC CENSUS 
BACKGROUND 
Ø The Sixth Economic Census (EC) is a 100% centrally sponsored scheme implemented by 
GoI across all States and Union Territories in collaboration with State ’s Directorate of 
Economics and Statistics. 
 
Ø The fieldwork in the State was conducted by-and-large during October, 2013 to April, 
2014. 
 
Ø All establishments engaged in various agricultural (excluding crop production and 
plantation)  and non-agricultural activities (excluding  public administration, defence and 
compulsory social security services) were enumerated. 
 
Ø Data for handicraft / handloom establishments were collected for the first time in this 
Economic Census. 
 
Ø Enumeration Blocks (EBs) of Population Census, 2011 were used as the primary 
geographical units for collection of data. 
 
Ø In all, about 87 thousand enumerators and supervisors were engaged to collect the data 
from about 2.21 lakh EBs. 
 
ESTABLISHMENTS  
Ø As per the Sixth Economic Census (2013), 61.37 lakh establishments were found to be in 
operation, of which 32.94 lakh establishments (53.68%) were in rural areas and 28.43 
lakh establishments (46.32%) were in urban areas. The State stands second in India 
after Uttar Pradesh; in number of establishments. 
 
Ø Out of 61.37 lakh establishments, about 74.06% establishments (45.45lakh) were 
engaged in non-agricultural activities (excluding public administration, defence and 
compulsory social security activities), while the remaining 25.94% establishments 
(15.92lakh) were engaged in agricultural activities (excluding crop production and 
plantation). 
 
Ø Over an intervening period of about 8 years between Fifth EC and Sixth EC, the total 
number of establishments in the State have increased from 41.53 lakh in 2005 
(EC2005*) to 61.37 lakh in 2013 (EC2013), registering a growth of 47.79%.  The 
growth in rural areas was 59.93% and in urban areas it was 35.85%. 
(* For meaningful comparison of Sixth and Fifth ECs public administration, defence & compulsory social 
security service activities reported in Fifth EC are not considered.) 
Sixth Economic Census 2013, Maharashtra
1
\n--- PDF PAGE 8 ---\nØ During the period between the two Economic Censuses (2005 & 2013), non-agricultural 
establishments grew at the rate of  28.10%, while agricultural establishments grew at the 
rate of 163.53%. 
 
Ø Out of the total establishments, 26.04% belonged to the primary sector of which 
agricultural sector constitutes 25.94% and mining & quarrying constitutes 0.10% while 
secondary and tertiary sector constitute 16.98% and 56.98% establishments respectively. 
 
Ø Own Account Establishments (i.e. establishments without any hired worker) were 44.14 
lakh (71.92%) and the remaining 17.23 lakh (28.08%) establishments were with at least 
one hired worker. Own Account Establishments grew at the rate of 75.20% while the 
growth of establishments with hired workers was meager i.e.1.03% over Fifth EC. 
 
Ø Out of 61.37 lakh establishments, around 96.90% establishments were of private 
ownership and remaining 3.10% establishments belonged to Government or PSU.  
 
Ø More than one third (38.86%) of all the establishments in the State were household 
establishments (inside household), 14.23% establishments were operating from outside 
household without fixed structure and the remaining 46.91% establishments were 
operating from outside households with fixed structure. 
 
Ø Majority of the establishments (94.4%) were perennial in nature. Around 5% of the 
establishments were seasonal and remaining 0.6 % of the establishments were casual in 
nature. 
 
Ø Top five districts viz. Kolhapur (9.30%), Pune (8.38%), Mumbai Suburban (7.58%), 
Thane (7.15%) and Satara (6.25%) together accounted for about 38.67% of the total 
number of establishments in the State.  
 
Ø Livestock was the major economic activity (91.98%) of agricultural sector. Whereas, non-
agricultural sector was dominated by Retail Trade (33.13%) followed by Manufacturing 
(20.25%).   
 
Ø Maximum increase in number of establishments over Fifth EC was observed in Satara 
(169.53%) followed by Solapur (141.09%) and Osmanabad (88.67%). 
PROPRIETARY ESTABLISHMENTS  
Ø 90.76% of the establishments were owned by proprietors of which,11.93% were owned 
by females.  
 
Ø The establishments owned by Hindus were 69.19%, followed by 10.44% Muslims, 2.37% 
by Buddhists, 0.50% by Christians and the rest 8.26% by the other religions.

Sixth Economic Census 2013, Maharashtra
\n--- PDF PAGE 9 ---\nEMPLOYMENT  
Ø About 145.12 lakh persons were found employed in 61.37 lakh establishments.  The 
State stands first in providing employment at All India level . Out of the total 
employment, 41.77% were in rural areas and 58.23% in urban areas.  Similarly, out of the 
total employment, 78.14% were male and 21.86% were female.  The growth rate of 
employment over Fifth EC was 37.85%. 
 
Ø Employment in own account establishments was around of 59.05 lakh persons (40.69%). 
The employment in establishments with at least one hired worker was about 86.07 lakh 
persons (59.31%).  Agricultural establishments provided employment to about  26.39 lakh 
persons (18.18%) and the non-agricultural establishments provided employment to about 
118.73 lakh persons (81.82%). 
 
Ø The percentage of workers engaged in proprietary establishments was 78.63%, followed 
by 15.04% in Private companies/SHGs/Cooperatives etc. and rest 6.33% were employed 
in Government or Public sector undertakings. 
 
Ø Manufacturing sector was the largest employer providing employment to 29.18 lakh 
(20.10%) persons. This was followed by retail trade employing 26.73 lakh persons 
(18.42%) and livestock sector employing 24.11 lakh persons (16.61%). 
 
Ø Distribution of establishments by size-class of employment  reveals that around 58.19 
lakh establishments (94.81%) were having 1 to 5 workers, around 2.38 lakh 
establishments (3.88%) were having 6 to 9 workers, while 0.8 lakh establishments 
(1.31%) employed 10 or more workers. 
 
Ø The top five districts viz. Mumbai Suburban (11.51%), Pune (10.99%), Thane (9.95%), 
Kolhapur (7.58%) and Mumbai city (7.14%) accounted for almost half of the total 
employment in the State. 
 
Ø Highest growth rate in employment was observed in Satara (155.79%), followed by 
Solapur (76.17%) and Sangli (73.53%). 
 
Ø Overall average employment per establishment in Sixth EC was 2.36, as against 2.68 in 
Fifth EC.  Average employment per establishment in Sixth EC was 1.34 for Own Account 
Establishments and 4.99 in case of establishments with at least one hired worker. 
WOMEN ENTREPRENEURS 
Ø The number of establishments owned by women entrepreneurs was 6.64 lakh (10.82%). 
These establishments provided employment to 10.98 lakh persons (7.57%). Of which, 
64.13% were without hired workers.  
 
Highlights of the Sixth Economic Census

\n--- PDF PAGE 10 ---\nØ Of the total establishments under women entrepreneurs, about 26.86% belonged to 
agricultural activities, with livestock dominating with a share of 25.26%.  Among non-
agricultural activities owned by women entrepreneurs, manufacturing and retail trade 
were the dominant ones, corresponding percentages being 27.02% and 20.63% 
respectively.  
 
Ø Of the total establishments owned by women entrepreneurs 23.29% were owned by OBC, 
4.49% by SC, and 11.80% by ST. 
 
Ø Of the total establishments owned by women entrepreneurs by religion, it was observed 
that 71.32% were owned by Hindus, 8.47% by Muslims, 4.03% by Buddhist and 1.05% 
by Christians.  
 
Ø The top five districts by number of establishments under women entrepreneurship were 
Kolhapur (15.07%), followed by Satara (8.19%), Pune (7.97%), Solapur (7.62%) and 
Mumbai Suburban (7.29%). 
 
Ø Average employment per female owned establishment was found to be 1.65. 
HANDICRAFT/HANDLOOM ESTABLISHMENTS 
Ø The number of Handicraft/Handloom establishments was 0.85 lakh (1.38%). These 
establishments provided employment to 2.15 lakh persons (1.48%). Of which, 67.02% 
were without hired workers.  
 
Ø Majority of establishments (96.90%) were owned by proprietors, of which, 16.16% 
establishments were owned by females.  
 
Ø Nearly 73.13% of proprietary establishments of handicrafts/handloom were owned by 
Hindus, 16.21% by Muslims and 0.43% by Christians. Of these proprietary 
establishments, 29.54% were owned by OBCs, 12.90% by SCs and 4.84% by STs. 
 
Ø Among the districts, Mumbai suburban had the largest share (13.34%) followed by 
Mumbai City (11.87%), Kolhapur (11.33%), Thane (9.12%) and Solapur (5.43%). 
 
Ø Average employment per establishment for handicraft/handloom establishments was 2.54.

\n--- PDF PAGE 13 ---\nCHAPTER I  
INTRODUCTION 
1.1  Background 
Economic Census (EC) is the complete count of all establishments (engaged in 
production and / or distribution of goods and services not for the sole purpose of own 
consumption) located within the geographical boundaries of the country. The Economic 
Census is a 100% centrally sponsored scheme implemented by Central Statistics Office 
(CSO), GoI in collaboration with State ’s Directorate of Economics and Statistics. In the past, 
Economic Censuses were conducted in 1977, 1980, 1990, 1998 and 2005. The Sixth 
Economic Census was conducted by-and-large during October, 2013 to April, 2014 in the 
State by the Directorate of Economics and Statistics, Planning Department, Government of 
Maharashtra. General information about Sixth Economic Census in brief based on 
publications of CSO, GoI is given below. The detailed information is available on GoI 
website www.mospi.nic.in 
 After independence, Government of India has adopted the principle of planned 
development in all sectors of economy. As a result, the role of planning has become vital in 
the overall development of the country. For planning, availability of updated statistical data 
pertaining to different sectors of economy is a prerequisite.   The CSO, GoI and Directorate 
of Economics & Statistics (DES) of the State Government are playing key role in collection 
and compilation of basic statistical data.  Data upto certain extent is available in agricultural 
sector and organised non-agricultural sector.  However, there are considerable data gaps in 
respect of unorganised non-agricultural sector, mainly because of its scattered nature. With a 
view to bridge the data gaps in the unorganised segments of different sectors of the economy, 
economic censuses are being conducted by the CSO, GoI.  The other important purpose of 
conducting economic census is to generate an updated frame of establishments for detailed 
follow up surveys.  It yields essential data on number and distribution of establishments 
engaged in different types of economic activities forming the basis for the follow-up surveys.  
Economic censuses have provided basic entrepreneurial data relating to the unorganised 
segments of different sectors of the economy; for the purpose of planning, development, 
administration & improvement of national income and other related aggregate estimates.  
1.2  First Economic Census - 1977 
 The First Economic Census was conducted during 1977 throughout the country, 
except Lakshadweep. The census was conducted in collaboration with the DES in the 
States/Union Territories. The coverage was restricted to only non-agricultural establishments 
employing atleast one hired worker on a fairly regular basis. The information on location of 
enterprise, description of economic activity carried on, nature of operation, type of 
Sixth Economic Census 2013, Maharashtra
7
\n--- PDF PAGE 14 ---\nownership, social group of owner, use of power/fuel, total number of workers usually 
engaged with its hired components and with break-up of male and female workers were 
collected in this economic census. 
1.3  Second Economic Census-1980 
 The Second Economic Census was conducted in 1980 along with the house-listing 
operation of 1981 Population Census.  The scope and coverage of this census was larger than 
the first census. That time, all establishments engaged in economic activities both agricultural 
and non-agricultural (except those engaged in crop production and plantation) were 
employing any workers or not was covered.  All States/Union Territories were covered with 
the sole exception of Assam, where Population Census 1981 was not conducted. The items, 
on which information was collected in Second Economic Census, were more or less similar to 
First Economic Census. However, based on experience gained in the First Economic Census 
certain items viz. years of activity, value of annual output/turnover/receipts, mixed activity or 
not, registered / licensed / recognized and act or authority, if registered were dropped. 
1.4  Third Economic Census-1990 
 The Third Economic Census was synchronised with the house-listing operation of the 
Population Census 1991 on the same pattern of EC 1980. All States/UTs except Jammu & 
Kashmir, where Population Census 1991 was not undertaken were covered.  It was a joint 
effort of CSO and the Register General of India in collaboration with the DES of the 
States/UTs.  The item coverage of this census was the same as that of Second Economic 
Census conducted in 1980. 
1.5  Fourth Economic Census-1998 
 With a view to bridge data gaps emerged due to adoption of liberalisation policy and 
to meet the requirements of various user departments, the Fourth Economic Census was 
conducted in 1998. This economic census was delinked from population census and was 
organised and conducted independently by CSO in collaboration with the DES of the 
States/UTs. 
 In the Fourth Economic Census complete enumeration of all agricultural (except crop 
production and plantation) and non-agricultural entrepreneurial activities was carried out 
throughout the country.  The overall responsibility for organisation and conduct of census 
rested with CSO. The DES of respective States/UTs was made responsible for conducting the 
fieldwork and preparation of the reports. 
 Fourth Economic Census operations envisaged identification of the establishments 
through complete house listing in House List Schedule and collecting the particulars about 
each establishments identified through the House List Schedule.  Further, the items of 
information collected in Fourth Economic Census included premises status, description of 
Introduction
8
Sixth Economic Census 2013, Maharashtra
\n--- PDF PAGE 15 ---\nactivity, nature of operation, ownership type, social group of owner, power/fuel used, years of 
operation, registration, source of finance, total number of persons usually working and hired 
persons with break up in male/female and adult/child categories. 
1.6  Fifth Economic Census-2005  
 The National Statistical Commission (NSC) and Monitoring Committee for Fourth 
Economic Census had recommended for conducting the economic census once in 10 years.  
However, it was seen that method of conducting various follow-up surveys utilising ten years 
census as frame, resulted in loss on information, as during the period of ten years many 
industries disappear from the field while others comeup in various sectors.  Economic 
reforms and the policy of liberalisation, having huge impact on various sectors of the 
economy were in its initial stages; therefore, the Fourth Economic Census conducted in 1998 
could only partially capture the impact of economic reforms.  In fitness of this and to get data 
in structural & compositional changes in the economy, the Government conducted the Fifth 
Economic Census in 2005. 
1.7  Sixth Economic Census-2013  
1.7.1  Objectives 
Various censuses and surveys are being conducted to bridge data gaps.  As Economic 
Census covers unorganised sector both in manufacturing and services along with organised 
sector, it has significant importance in the statistical system of India.  The main objectives for 
the conduct of Sixth Economic Census are as below: 
i) To provide detailed information on operational and economic variables (activity wise) 
of all the establishments (excluding crop production, plantation, public administration, 
defense and compulsory social security services) of the country including its 
distribution at All-India, state and sub-state levels for comprehensive analysis of the 
structure of the economy and for benchmark purposes; to provide similar data at 
lower geographical levels like tahsils / villages in case of rural areas and towns / 
wards in case of urban areas for decentralised planning; 
ii) To generate information on all establishments, (including exporting units) concerning 
employment of 8 or more workers, activities/area wise operations.   
iii) To provide information on number of workers working in unorganised sector (i.e. 
establishments employing less than ten workers);  
 
Introduction
Sixth Economic Census 2013, Maharashtra
9
\n--- PDF PAGE 16 ---\niv) To provide  updated Directory of  Establishments employing 10 or more workers for 
local level planning purposes and also for using as a ‘List Frame ’ for conduct of 
survey of such establishments in the non-manufacturing / services sector;   
and 
v) To provide up-to-date ‘Area Frame ’ containing information on number of 
establishments and number of workers by industry, by type of ownership of the 
establishments, etc. at the village / block level, from which sample villages / blocks 
could be drawn as the primary sampling units for collecting detailed information 
about the establishments in the follow-up sample surveys of establishments. 
1.7.2  Scope and coverage 
All economic activities (except those involved in crop production & plantation, public 
administration, defence & compulsory social security services) related to production and / or 
distribution of goods and / or services (excluding for the sole purpose of own consumption) 
were covered in Sixth Economic Census. However, the following activities were kept out of 
the purview of the Sixth Economic Census. 
i) Establishments of shelter-less and nomadic population, which keep on moving from 
place to place and camp either without shelter or with makeshift shelter.  
ii) Establishments engaged in illegal activities like smuggling, gambling, beggary, 
prostitution, etc.  
iii) Domestic servants, whether they work in one household or in a number of 
households, drivers, etc. who undertake jobs for others on wages.  
iv) All wage-paid employees of casual nature.  
v) Household members engaged in household chores.  
vi) Persons doing different types of jobs depending on the availability of work e.g. 
loading, unloading, helping a mason or a carpenter, doing earthwork for a contractor.  
vii) Household members working for other households and earning some money which is 
insignificant.  
viii) Households in which none of the members are engaged in any gainful activity i.e. 
households depending on remittance, rent, interest, pension etc.  
Introduction
10
Sixth Economic Census 2013, Maharashtra
\n--- PDF PAGE 17 ---\n1.7.3  Planning & Organisation of Sixth EC 
 For conducting the Sixth Economic Census, a Standing Committee was constituted 
under the chairmanship of Director General, CSO to look into various aspects relating to the 
conduct of Sixth Economic Census and to provide guidance/directions.  The working groups 
were also constituted for the following aspects of Sixth Economic Census. 
a) To decide scope and coverage of the census. 
 
b) To design formats for various questionnaires/schedules; to write down conceptual and 
operational definitions of the items included in the schedules and to draft instruction 
manual for data collection for use of supervisors & enumerators. 
 
c) To estimate the total volume of field work in terms of (i) likely number of 
establishments to be enumerated, (ii) estimated strength of field staff needed, (iii) 
likely time required for completion of field work. 
 
d) To prepare a plan for tabulation of data and formats for the final tables,   
 
and 
 
e) Other administrative aspects like printing of schedule, dealing with fixing of time 
frame for receipt of schedules, appointing data processing agency at the Centre etc. 
The overall responsibility for organisation and conduct of the census was with CSO, 
while responsibility of fieldwork and supervision was given to the DES in the respective 
States/UTs. 
 In Maharashtra State, Additional Chief Secretary Planning Department (GoM) was 
overall in charge of the Sixth Economic Census operation and he was designated as Census 
Commissioner. The Director of DES was designated as Additional Census Commissioner.  
For carrying out the field work of the census in the districts, the District Collectors were 
designated as Joint Census Commissioner. Joint Directors of DES involved in census work 
were designated as Deputy Census Commissioner & District Statistical Officers were 
Assistant Census Commissioner. 
For implementation, monitoring and co-ordination of Sixth Economic Census in the 
State, three tier committees were constituted as follows. 
1. State level co-ordination committee (SLCC) headed by the Chief Secretary. 
2. District level co-ordination headed by District Collector. 
3. Taluka level co-ordination committee headed by Tahsildar. 
 
Introduction
Sixth Economic Census 2013, Maharashtra
11
\n--- PDF PAGE 18 ---\nIn addition to above, in the major cities having Municipal Corporations, 
committee was constituted under the chairmanship of respective Municipal 
Commissioners. The DES worked as the nodal agency for executing the Sixth Economic 
Census in the State. 
1.7.4  Training 
The CSO conducted training programmes on concepts, definitions and canvassing of 
schedules for the State officials involved in the economic census. Further, training  
programme for lower level officials like charge officers, enumerators and supervisors were 
organized by DES officials. 
1.7.5  Fieldwork 
 Enumeration Blocks (EBs) of Population Census 2011 were used as primary 
geographical units for both urban and rural areas for the purpose of listing of 
establishments.In previous Economic Census, UFS (Urban Frame Survey) Blocks of NSSO 
were taken as the primary geographical units for urban areas. Use of EBs would facilitate 
the linking of Census 2011 database with that of Sixth Economic Census at the lower 
geographical levels like EBs and wards. Abridged House List (AHL) and Layout Maps 
(LMs) of Census 2011 EBs were provided to the enumerators to facilitate proper coverage.  
i) 
Schedules canvassed 
In Sixth Economic Census two schedules were canvassed, viz. i) House and 
Establishment Listing Schedule (6A) and ii) Directory of Establishment Schedule 
(6C),in place of three schedules canvassed during Fifth Economic Census. House and 
Establishment Listing Schedule of Sixth Economic Census was formed by merging 
‘House List ’ and ‘Enterprise Schedule ’of the Fifth Economic Census . Further, the  
‘Address Slip ’ Schedule of Fifth Economic Census was modified and renamed as 
Directory of Establishment Schedule  in Sixth Economic Census for developing 
Business Register . 
 The third schedule ‘Establishment Abstract ’ (6B) was a derived schedule of 
summary information based on ‘House and Establishment Listing Schedule 6(A) ’.  
This summary information has been used to generate provisional results contained in 
this report.  Copies of these schedules / forms 6A, 6B & 6C are given in Annexure I, II 
& III respectively. 
ii)  Manpower utilised 
In order to cover about 2.23 lakh EBs in the State, about 59 thousand enumerators and 
28 thousand supervisors were engaged.  Comprehensive training for the trainers, providing 
further training to the field staff was imparted by DES Officials.

\n--- PDF PAGE 19 ---\n1.7.6  Field agency for enumeration & supervision 
 During Sixth Economic Census 2013, the responsibility of the conduct of field work 
was assigned to charge officers and their areas of operation were earmarked.  For urban and 
rural areas following officials were appointed as charge officers. 
Area Appointed as charge officers 
Urban Area 
1. Municipal Corporations Deputy Commissioner 
2. Municipal Council Chief Officer/ Executive Officer/ Administrator 
 3. Cantonment Area 
 
Officer of Commanding Station 
 Rural Area 
 4. Tahsil Level 
 
Tahsildar / NaibTahsildar 
 
 As per the decision taken in SLCC meeting, State Govt. employees, local body 
employees or educated unemployed youths were mostly engaged for the field work of Sixth 
Economic Census.  The appointment of enumerators and supervisors was done by the charge 
officers. 
1.7.7  Items of information collected 
 The items of information collected in the Sixth Economic Census included 
description of activity, premises status, nature of operation, ownership type, social 
group& religion of owner, power used,  registration code, source of finances, total no. of 
persons  usually working, hired and non-hired persons with break-up in male/female 
categories,  detail address of establishments having 8 or more workers employed.  
Information regarding handicraft/handloom establishments was also separately collected 
for the first time. 
1.7.8  Classification of activities 
 Economic activities were assigned 3 digit National Industrial Classification (NIC 
2008) codes. 
1.7.9  Data processing 
 The data /information collected through field work of the Sixth Economic Census 
was scrutinised and NIC codes were assigned in House and Establishment Listing 
Introduction
Sixth Economic Census 2013, Maharashtra
13
\n--- PDF PAGE 20 ---\nSchedule (6A) and Directory of Establishment Schedule (6C).  After pre-scanning work, 
schedules were sent to Data Centre, Faridabad for scanning and e-flow processing work.  
Intelligent Character Recognition (ICR) was used for scanning and processing Economic 
Census data.  CSO, GoI has provided final tables of economic census results to DES. 
1.7.10  Limitation of Sixth Economic Census 
i) Collection of information is not generally record-based but based on oral 
information.  The factors like recall lapse and casual approach on the part of 
informants may lead to some non-sampling errors. 
 
ii) Due to non-availability of teachers, the fieldwork was required to be assigned to 
persons who were new to such type of work. 
 
iii) Although certain improvements were made in the schedule to guard against 
under-listing of establishments (particularly the home-based units or those 
operating without fixed structure), data as per Sixth Economic Census may be 
subject to some amount of coverage error on account of such under-listing, which 
is difficult to eliminate totally in a massive census operation.

\n--- PDF PAGE 21 ---\nCHAPTER II 
RESULTS AT A GLANCE 
 
2.1  Establishments at all India level 
 As per the Sixth Economic Census (2013), 584.95 lakh establishments were found to 
be in operation at All India level.  Of which 59.48% establishments were found to be located 
in rural areas and nearly 40.52% establishments were found to be located in urban areas.  
Number of Own Account Establishments was 419.64 lakh (71.74%) and establishments with 
at least one hired worker were 165.31 lakh (28.26%). 
 Of 584.95 lakh establishments, about 77.6% establishments were engaged in               
non-agricultural activities (excluding public administration, defence and compulsory social 
security activities), while the remaining 22.4% establishments were found to be engaged in 
agricultural activities (excluding crop production and plantation). 
Top five States viz. Uttar Pradesh (11.43%), Maharashtra (10.49%), West Bengal 
(10.10%), Tamil Nadu (8.60%) and Andhra Pradesh (7.25%) together accounted for about 
50% of the total number of establishments in the country.  At all India level, Maharashtra 
ranked second.

\n--- PDF PAGE 23 ---\n2.2  Employment at all India level 
As per Sixth EC, about 1312.94 lakh persons were found employed in 584.95 lakh 
establishments at All India level.  Out of the total 1312.94 lakh persons, 51.71% persons were 
employed in rural areas and 48.29% persons were employed in urban areas.  
The employment in Own Account Establishments was 581.49 lakh persons (44.29%), 
whereas, the employment in establishments with at least one hired worker was about 731.45 
lakh persons (55.71%). Agricultural establishments provided employment to around 228.83 
lakh persons (17.42%) and the non-agricultural establishments provided employment to 
around 1084.11 lakh persons (82.58%). 
Top five States viz. Maharashtra (11.05%), Uttar Pradesh (10.75%), West Bengal 
(9.07%), Tamil Nadu (8.91%) and Gujarat (7.32%) accounted for almost half of the total 
employment in the country.  Though the Maharashtra ranked second in terms of number 
of establishments, it holds first rank in providing employment in the country.  The State 
wise total number of persons employed by sector is given in Table 2.2, and the results of the 
State and all India level are given in Table 2.3

\n--- PDF PAGE 26 ---\n2.3  Establishments in the State 
As per the results of the Sixth Economic Census, there were 61.37 lakh 
establishments in the State engaged in different economic activities other than crop 
production, plantation, public administration, defence and compulsory social security 
services. Out of which, 32.94 lakh establishments (53.68%) were in the rural areas and 28.43 
lakh establishments (46.32%) were in the urban areas. The top five districts having maximum 
number of establishments in the State are Kolhapur (9.30%), Pune (8.38%), Mumbai 
(7.58%), Thane (7.15%) and Satara (6.25%).  In the State, 44.14lakh (71.92%) establishments 
were Own Account Establishments (OAE) i.e. establishments without any hired worker. Of 
the total OAE, 27.71 lakh (62.78%) were in rural areas and 16.43 lakh (37.22%) were in 
urban areas. The remaining 17.23 lakh (28.08%) establishments were with at least one hired 
worker, out of which, 5.23 lakh (30.36%) were in rural areas and 12 lakh (69.64%) were in 
urban areas. 
Among these establishments, 45.45 lakh (74.06%) were engaged in non-agricultural 
activities (excluding public administration, defence and compulsory social security services) 
while the rest 15.92 lakh (25.94%) were engaged in agricultural activities other than crop 
production and plantation.

establishments and the remaining 12 lakh (42.22%) establishments were with at least one

establishments operating from outside household without fixed structure, 4.21 lakh (48.18%) 
were in rural areas and remaining 4.52 lakh (51.82%) were in urban areas. Out of 23.85 lakh 
establishments located inside household, 17.78 lakh (74.56%) were in rural areas and 
remaining 6.07 lakh (25.44%) were in urban areas.

female employment 50.43% was in rural areas and 49.57% in urban areas.  Among the hired 
workers, 14.23 lakh (20.47 %) were females.

\n--- PDF PAGE 32 ---\n2.5  Establishments by major activity groups 
Activities pursued by various establishments, were grouped into 23 major activity 
groups (four for agricultural activities and 19 for non-agricultural activities).  Among the 
agricultural establishments, livestock dominated with its total share of 91.98% (14.64 lakh).  
Further, 96.53% (14.13 lakh) of the total establishments involved in livestock were found in 
the rural areas. 
‘Retail trade’, ‘Manufacturing’ and ‘Other service activities not elsewhere classified 
(including membership organization, repair of computers and personnel household goods) ’ 
were the three most important activity groups in terms of number of establishments in the               
non-agricultural sector.  These three activity groups together shared about 63.18% of the total 
establishments.  The group ‘retail trade’ alone with a share of 33.13% and numbering a total 
of around 15.06 lakh establishments dominated all other groups. It was followed by 
‘manufacturing’ and ‘other service activities not elsewhere classified (including membership 
organization, repair of computers and personnel household goods) ’ with share of  20.25% 
(9.20 lakh) and 9.80 % (4.46 lakh) respectively.  The number of establishments and persons 
employed by broad activity groups were given in Table 2.6. 
 
2.6  Employment in establishments by major activity groups  
Among the agricultural activities, the maximum employment was found under the 
activity group ‘livestock’ with 24.10 lakh workers constituting 91.36% of the total 
employment in the agricultural sector. 
In non-agricultural activities, the maximum number of workers were found engaged 
in the ‘manufacturing’ with 29.18 lakh workers (24.57%), followed by ‘retail trade ’ with 
26.73 lakh(22.51%) and ‘education’ with 10.38 lakh (8.75%). These three activity groups 
taken together accounted for about 55.83% of  the total employment in non-agricultural 
sector.  Almost similar trend of employment was observed in rural and urban establishments. 
Thepercentage share of employment in these three activity groups viz ‘manufacturing’, ‘retail 
trade’ and ‘education’ in rural area was 26.10%, 23.77% and 14.27% respectively.  In urban 
areas, activity group ‘manufacturing’ employed the maximum number of persons (23.92% of 
total urban employment), followed by ‘retail trade’ (21.97%) and ‘education’ (6.39%).

2.8  Other selected characteristics 
 It was observed that out of 61.37 lakh establishments in the State,1.90 lakh 
establishments (3.10%) were under Government ownership, 55.70 lakh (90.76%) under 
private proprietorship, 0.45lakh (0.74%) under private partnership, 0.46 lakh (0.76%) under

(2.76%) under other types of  private establishments.

Sixth Economic Census 2013, Maharashtra
\n--- PDF PAGE 37 ---\nCHAPTER III 
AGRICULTURAL ESTABLISHMENTS 
3.1 General 
Agricultural establishments (including agriculture, livestock, forestry and fishery) play a 
vital role in the State economy.  Agricultural establishments engaged in production of 
agricultural goods (other than crop production & plantation by the farmers or a group of farmers 
or any agency), agricultural services, hunting, trapping & related activities, where at least some 
part of the production or services was sold out were covered in Sixth EC. Establishments 
engaged in activities pertaining to crop production and plantations, were not covered in Sixth EC 
as these are being covered regularly under Agricultural Census since 1970-71. Thus, primarily 
cultivators were excluded from the census. However, services incidental to crop production or 
plantation provided/ undertaken by any one individual or a firm or a company by charging fee or 
rent/compensation (e.g. machinery & equipment for tilling/cultivation, preparation of field or 
sowing harvesting/chaffing, transportation of agricultural goods/produce, charging for irrigation 
facilities etc. as their principal activity) were included and the establishments engaged in such 
activities were enumerated.  Further, establishments engaged in Livestock (animal production, 
support activities for animal production and hunting, trapping and related service activities), 
Forestry, Logging, Fishing and Aquaculture (marine fishing, fresh water fishing, and marine and 
fresh water aquaculture) were also covered under agriculture in Sixth EC. 
Data pertaining to agricultural establishments covered under Sixth Economic Census are 
presented in this chapter. 
3.2 Establishments 
According to Sixth Economic Census (EC) there were 15.92 lakh agricultural 
establishments accounting to 25.94% of total establishments in the State.  
About 95.67% (15.23 lakh) agricultural establishments were located in rural areas. Of 
which, 95.51% were own account establishments (establishments without any hired workers) and 
rest were with at least one hired worker. In urban areas, own account establishments shared the 
major chunk (85.68%) of the establishments in the agricultural establishments. 
Broad activity wise classification of agricultural establishments shows that maximum 
percentage of  91.98% establishments were engaged in livestock activity, followed by 5.40% in 
Sixth Economic Census 2013, Maharashtra
31
\n--- PDF PAGE 38 ---\nother than crop production & plantation, 2.17% fishing and aquaculture and 0.45% forestry and 
logging. The share of establishments engaged in livestock in rural areas was 92.8%, followed by 
agriculture other than crop production & plantation (5.19%), fishing and aquaculture (1.67%) 
and forestry & logging (0.33%).  In case of urban areas this distribution was livestock (73.79%), 
followed by fishing & aquaculture (13.14%), agriculture other than crop production & plantation 
(10.0%) and forestry & logging (3.06%). 
3.3 Employment 
The agricultural establishments engaged 26.39 lakh persons, which constituted 18.18% of 
the total employment. Of these, 23.53 lakh (89.18%) persons were employed in own account 
establishments and about 2.86 lakh (10.82%) in establishments with at least one hired worker. 
About 95.20% of the employment in agricultural establishments, numbering 25.12 lakh 
worked in rural areas. Out of these, 90.21% of the persons worked in own account 
establishments and rest in establishments with at least one hired worker. 
The share of employment in the agricultural establishments by major broad activity 
shows that 91.36% workers were engaged in livestock, followed by 5.82% in agriculture other 
than crop production & plantation, 2.22% fishing and aquaculture and 0.6% forestry and logging.  
The share of employment for establishments engaged in livestock in rural areas was 92.44%, 
followed by agriculture other than crop production & plantation (5.52%), fishing and aquaculture 
(1.62%) and forestry and logging (0.41%). Whereas in urban areas was livestock (69.97%), 
followed by fishing and aquaculture (13.98%), agriculture other than crop production & 
plantation (11.69%) and forestry & logging (4.36%). 
About 96.29% of the female workers in agricultural establishments numbering 8.64 lakh 
were found working in rural areas, and 3.71% of the female workers in agricultural 
establishments numbering 0.33 lakh were found working in urban areas.  
At the State level, average employment of 1.66 persons per establishment was observed 
in both the rural and the urban areas for agricultural establishments. In the own account 
establishments, this average was 1.55 persons per establishment and for establishments with at 
least one hired worker, it was 3.65 persons per establishment.

\n--- PDF PAGE 43 ---\n3.5.1  Agricultural establishments under proprietary ownership by selected  
characteristics 
The majority 15.07 lakh (95.75%) of agricultural establishments under proprietary 
ownership were in rural areas employing about 24.63 lakh workers (95.37%), whereas the 
remaining about 0.67 lakh establishments (4.25%) were in urban areas which employed about 
1.19 lakh (4.63%) workers.  The average employment in the agricultural establishments was 1.64 
persons per establishment at State level, whereas it was 1.63 in rural areas and 1.79 in urban 
areas.  The proprietary agricultural establishments under male, female and others ownership were 
13.87 lakh (88.13%), 1.78 lakh (11.34%) and 0.08 lakh (0.53%) respectively that employed 
23.16 lakh (89.68%), 2.49 lakh (9.63%) and 0.18 lakh (0.68%) workers at State level.  Similar 
trend was observed in rural areas where the distribution of male, female and others ownership 
was 13.30 lakh (88.25%), 1.69 lakh (11.22%) and 0.08 lakh (0.53%) respectively, whereas in 
urban areas the same was about 0.57 lakh (85.43%), 0.09 lakh (14.04%) and 0.004 lakh (0.53%) 
respectively. 
At State level, the average employment under male, female and other ownership was 
1.67, 1.39 and 2.10 persons per establishment respectively.  In the rural areas, the average 
employment of persons per establishment under male, female and other ownership was 1.66, 
1.40 and 2.10 respectively, whereas this distribution in urban areas was 1.85, 1.37 and 2.27 
respectively.

\n--- PDF PAGE 44 ---\n3.6  Social group of owners  
In rural area, the social group ‘Others’ (i.e. other than SC/ST/OBC) owned 64.16% of 
agricultural establishments, which engaged 65.32% of the total agricultural establishment ’s work 
force. On the other hand, the ‘Others’ group of urban area owned 57.05% of these establishments 
with 59.79% employment therein.  At State level the share of social group ‘Others’ in 
agricultural establishments was 63.86 and employment therein was 65.06%. 
The SC and ST in the State together owned 12.66% of the total agricultural 
establishments where 12.59% of the workers were engaged.  An agricultural establishment 
owned by OBC owners was 23.49% of total agricultural establishments having the employment 
share of 22.36% in the State.  In urban areas, 29.06% establishments were under OBCs ’ 
ownership that employed 27.60% of the workers. The share of OBC ownership of proprietary 
agricultural establishments in rural areas was 23.24% employing 22.10% workers.

\n--- PDF PAGE 45 ---\n3.7  Religion of owners 
At State level, Hindus owned 88.64% of the agricultural establishments which employed 
88.99% of the workforce.  This distribution was 89.27% establishments employing 89.68% of 
workers and 74.6% establishments employing 74.64% of workers for rural and urban areas 
respectively. 
In rural areas, the Muslim owned 2.45% of agricultural establishments where 2.32% of the 
total agricultural establishments work force was engaged, whereas in urban areas Muslims owned 
11.63% establishments with 11.49% employment.  The Muslim owners owned 2.84% of 
establishments at State level with an employment share of 2.74%. 
The proprietary owners with religion as ‘others’ owned 5.18% of the establishments at State 
level, whereas it was 5.08% and 7.56% in rural and urban areas respectively.  The employment share 
for these establishments was 5.05% at State level and 4.95% & 7.13% in rural & urban areas 
respectively.

3.12  Inter-district comparison of agricultural establishments 
It was revealed that Kolhapur had the maximum number of agricultural establishments 
(3.26 lakh) constituting 20.48% of all agricultural establishments in the State, followed by Satara 
(13.96%), Solapur (13.42%), Sangali (11.49%) and Pune (6.97%).  
In case of own account agricultural establishments, the maximum share was of Kolhapur 
(20.95%), followed by Satara (14.32%), Solapur  (13.89%), Sangli (11.64%) and Ahmadnagar 
(6.79%). However, in case of agricultural establishments with at least one hired worker the 
maximum number was in Pune (14.93%) followed by Kolhapur (11.26%), Sangli (8.54%), 
Ahmadnagar (7.54%) and Akola (7.33%).  District wise agricultural establishments by their type 
and location have been given in Tables 3.11.

\n--- PDF PAGE 51 ---\nAmong the broad activities constituting agricultural establishments, Akola was at the top 
with 39.78% (0.34 lakh) of total establishments engaged in activities relating to agriculture other 
than crop production & plantation, followed by Satara 12.84% (0.11 lakh) and Kolhapur 7.46% 
(0.06 lakh).  
Majority of agricultural establishments were engaged in livestock at State level, with 
Kolhapur at the top with 3.18 lakh (21.70%) establishments, followed by Solapur 2.11 lakh 
(14.42%) and Satara 2.09 lakh (14.31%). 
A total of about 0.35 lakh establishments were engaged in fishery and aquaculture at 
State level, with Ratnagiri at the top with 0.043 lakh (12.47%) establishments, followed by 
Raigad 0.041 lakh (11.81%) and Sindhudurg 0.027 lakh (7.38%). 
The employment in the agricultural establishments reveals that Kolhapur leads in 
providing employment in agricultural establishments with 5.63 lakh workers constituting 21.33% 
of the total employment in agricultural establishments. The districts with more than 10% share in 
total employment in agricultural establishments were Satara (13.32%), Sangli (12.52%) and 
Solapur (10.89%).  The details are given in Tables 3.12. 
In agricultural own account establishments, the maximum share in the employment was 
of Kolhapur (22.81%), followed by Satara (14.29%) and Sangli (12.59%).  However, in the case 
of agricultural establishments with at least one hired worker, the highest employment was found 
in the Pune constituting 14.67%, followed by Sangli (11.93%) and Kolhapur (9.11%). 
The employment in agricultural establishments in rural areas and urban areas, Kolhapur 
district was the highest with 5.46 lakh and 0.16 lakh workers respectively. 
The employment in the agricultural establishments by broad activities shows that, Akola 
with 36.9% (0.57 lakh) of total workers engaged in activities relating to agriculture other than 
crop production & plantation was at the top, followed by Satara 9.82% (0.15 lakh) and Kolhapur 
6.85% (0.11 lakh). 
The majority of workers in agricultural establishments were engaged in livestock with 
Kolhapur at the top with 5.49 lakh (22.77%) of total workers, followed by Satara 3.34 lakh 
(13.85%) and Sangli 3.28 lakh (13.59%). 
Out of the total 0.16 lakh workers engaged in forestry and logging establishments, the top 
three districts in terms of number of workers were Akola (14.69%), Kolhapur (12.68%) and 
Gondiya (11.64%) respectively. 
About 0.58 lakh workers were engaged in fishery and aquaculture establishments.  
Raigad with about 0.09 lakh workers (15.53%) was at the top, followed by Ratnagiri (13.8%) 
and Sindhudurg (9.2%).

Sixth Economic Census 2013, Maharashtra
\n--- PDF PAGE 53 ---\nCHAPTER IV 
NON-AGRICULTURAL ESTABLISHMENTS 
4.1  Coverage 
A non-agricultural establishment for the purpose of Sixth Economic Census has been 
defined as an establishment which was engaged in mining and quarrying; manufacturing 
(including repair & installation of machinery and equipment); electricity, gas, steam and air 
conditioning supply; water supply, sewerage, waste management and remediation activities; 
construction; wholesale trade, retail trade & repair of motor vehicles and motorcycles; other 
wholesale trade; other retail trade; transport and storage (including postal and courier 
activities); accommodation and food service activities; information and communication 
(including computer programming, consultancy and related services); financial and insurance 
activities; real estate activities; professional, scientific and technical activities (including 
advertising & market research and veterinary activities); administrative and support service 
activities (including travel agency, employment activities, security services, activities of call 
centers and organization of conventions and trade shows); education; human health and social 
work activity (including residential and non-residential care centers); arts, entertainment, 
sports and amusement and recreation (excluding illegal gambling and betting activities), other 
service activities not elsewhere classified (including membership organization, repair of 
computers and personnel household goods). In short establishments other than the 
agricultural establishments are treated as non-agricultural establishments. 
4.2 Establishments 
There were 45.46 lakh non-agricultural establishments (38.97% in rural areas and 
61.03% in urban areas) in the State, which accounted for 74.06% of the total establishments. 
Out of these, 29 lakh (63.80%) were own account establishments and the remaining about 
16.45 lakh (36.20%) were establishments with at least one hired worker. 
Out of 17.71 lakh non-agricultural establishments located in rural areas, 13.16 lakh 
were own account establishments and remaining 4.55 lakh establishments were with at least 
one hired worker. Out of 27.74 lakh non-agricultural establishments in the urban area, 15.84 
lakh were own account establishments and the remaining 11.90 lakh establishments were 
with at least one hired worker. 
4.3  Employment 
About 118.73 lakh persons worked in non-agricultural establishments, constituting 
about 81.82% of the total employment. Of these, 35.52 lakh persons (29.91%) were 
employed in own account establishments and the remaining 83.22 lakh (70.09%) were 
employed in establishments with at least one hired worker. Of the total employment in non-
agricultural sector, about 35.5 lakh (29.9%) was in rural areas, and 83.23 lakh (70.1%) was in 
urban areas. 
Sixth Economic Census 2013, Maharashtra
47
\n--- PDF PAGE 54 ---\nTotal employment of female workers in non-agricultural establishment was 22.75 
lakh (19.16%).  
There were 67.68 lakh hired workers working in the non-agricultural establishments 
which constitute 57% of total workers in the non-agricultural sector.  
Average employment of 2.61 persons per establishment was observed in non-
agricultural establishments. It was found to be higher in urban areas (3.00) than in rural areas 
(2.00).  Own account establishments and establishments with at least one hired worker had an 
average employment of 1.22 persons and 5.06 persons per establishment respectively.

\n--- PDF PAGE 56 ---\n4.4.1 Non-agricultural own account establishments by major activity  
groups 
There were 29.00 lakh own account establishments, constituting 63.80% of the non-
agricultural establishments. Amongst own account establishments, the activity group ‘retail 
trade’ (37.71%) had the highest share, followed by ‘manufacturing’ (20.38%) and ‘other 
service activities not elsewhere classified (including membership organization, repair of 
computers and personnel household goods)’ (11.28%).  
In the rural areas, ‘retail trade’ accounted for 37.43% of the total non-agricultural own 
account establishments, followed by ‘manufacturing’ and ‘other service activities not 
elsewhere classified (including membership organization, repair of computers and personnel 
household goods)’ sharing 23.93% and 11.65% of the establishments respectively. 
In urban areas the activity group ‘retail trade ’ had the largest share of 37.94% 
followed by ‘manufacturing’ with 17.43% share and ‘other service activities not elsewhere 
classified (including membership organization, repair of computers and personnel household 
goods)’ with share of 10.97 % in the total non-agricultural own account establishments. In 
rural and urban areas the three activity groups showed almost similar trends. 
4.4.2  Non-agricultural establishments with at least one hired worker by  
major activity groups 
There were 36.20 lakh non-agricultural establishments with at least one hired worker 
in the State, which constituted 36.19% of the total non-agricultural establishments. It is 
revealed that ‘retail trade ’(25.07%), ‘manufacturing’(20.01%) and ‘education’(9.49%) were 
the three most significant major activities which constitute 54.57% of the total non-
agricultural establishments.  
In the rural areas, activity group ‘education’ had the highest share of 24.49% in the 
total non-agricultural establishments having at least one hired worker, followed by ‘retail 
trade’ and ‘manufacturing’ with share of 19.54% and 12.86% respectively.  In urban areas 
activity group ‘retail trade’ was at the top with share of 27.19%, followed by ‘manufacturing’ 
sector 22.75% and ‘accommodation & food service’ with share of 7.26%. 
4.5  Employment by major activity groups 
In non-agricultural establishments, the maximum number of workers were found 
engaged in ‘manufacturing’ with 29.18 lakh workers (24.57%) in the State, followed by 
‘retail trade ’ with 26.73 lakh workers (22.51%) and ‘education’ with 10.38 lakh workers 
(8.75%).  These three activity groups together accounted for about 56% of the total 
employment in non-agricultural sector.  Similar trend of employment was also observed in 
rural & urban establishments. The details regarding the same are given in Table 4.3.

Sixth Economic Census 2013, Maharashtra
51
\n--- PDF PAGE 58 ---\n4.5.1 Employment in non-agricultural own account establishments by  
major activity groups 
In the own account establishments about 35.52 lakh persons were employed, which 
account for 29.91% of the total employment in non-agricultural establishments.  In rural areas 
about 45.01% (15.99 lakh) of the total employment were engaged in own account 
establishments. Amongst the own account establishments, the total employment in the non-
agricultural activities was dominated by activity group ‘retail trade ’ with employment of 
13.55 lakh (38.15%) followed by ‘manufacturing’ with 7.46 lakh (20.99%) employment.  
In rural and urban areas, employment in own account establishments followed the 
same trend. In rural areas activity group ‘retail trade ’ and ‘manufacturing’ accounted for 
37.46% and 24.17% and in urban areas, these groups accounted for 38.71% and 18.39% of 
the employment respectively. 
4.5.2 Employment in non-agricultural establishments with at least one  
hired worker by major activity groups 
Non-agricultural establishments with at least one hired worker gave employment to 
83.22 lakh persons. Activity group wise establishments with at least one hired worker shows, 
‘manufacturing’ activity accounts for 21.72 lakh (26.1%) of total employment, followed by 
‘retail trade ’ engaging 13.18 lakh workers (15.83%) and ‘education’ 9.89 lakh workers 
(11.89%). 
Of the total employment in the non-agricultural establishments with at least one hired 
worker about 76.55% was found in the urban areas and 23.45% in the rural areas.  
In rural areas, the activity group ‘manufacturing’ accounted for 27.68% of the total 
employment with 5.40 lakh workers, followed by ‘education’ and ‘retail trade’ with 4.93 lakh 
(25.27%) and 2.45 lakh (12.56%) of employment respectively. 
In urban areas, the activity group ‘manufacturing’ dominated with 16.32 lakh persons 
(25.62%), followed by the ‘retail trade ’ 10.73 lakh (16.84%) and ‘education’ 4.96 lakh 
(7.79%). 
 
4.6  Employment rate (Average employment per establishments) 
4.6.1   Employment rate in own account establishments 
It has been observed that, the rate of employment in own account establishments was 
1.22 persons, which was 1.21 in rural areas and 1.23 in urban areas. Employment rate per 
establishment in the State for own account establishments was highest in the ‘financial & 
insurance activities’ (1.63), followed by ‘accommodation and food service activities ’ (1.39) 
& ‘mining and quarrying ’ (1.36) respectively. Employment rate was found least (1.08) in

\n--- PDF PAGE 60 ---\n4.6.2 Employment rate in establishment with at least one hired worker 
Average employment in non-agricultural establishments with at least one hired 
worker for all the activities taken together was found to be 5.06 persons (4.29 in rural, 5.35 in 
urban). It was highest in activity group ‘information & communication (including computer 
programming, consultancy and related services) ’ (15.22), followed by ‘electricity, gas, steam 
and air conditioning supply’ (10.86) and   ‘mining & quarrying’ (9.40). Employment rate was 
least (2.71) in ‘Other service activities not elsewhere classified (including membership 
organization, repair of computers and personnel household goods) ’. In rural areas, the highest 
employment rate was found in ‘manufacturing’ (9.23), whereas in urban areas ‘information & 
communication (including computer programming, consultancy and related services) ’ had 
highest employment rate (18.06). 
 
4.7  Type of Ownership 
4.7.1  Non-agricultural establishments by type of ownership 
Out of 45.46 lakh non-agricultural establishments in the State, highest 39.96 lakh 
(87.91%) were under private proprietorship, followed by 1.87 lakh establishments (4.11%) 
under Government/PSU, 1.58 lakh (3.47%) under private others, 0.86 lakh (1.88%) under 
private non-profit institution, 0.46 lakh (1.01%) under private company, 0.44 lakh (0.97%) 
under private partnership, 0.18 lakh (0.40%) under private co-operative and 0.11 lakh 
(0.24%) under private self-help groups.  
Of the 16.45 lakh non-agricultural establishments with at least one hired worker in the 
State, majority 12.50 lakh (75.98%) were under private proprietorship, followed by 1.87 lakh 
(11.36%) under Government/PSU, 0.86 lakh (5.21%) under private others,  0.41 lakh (2.49%) 
under private company, 0.33 lakh (2.02%) under private partnership, 0.31 lakh (1.87%) under 
private non-profit institutions, 0.16 lakh (0.98%) under private cooperative, and 0.02 lakh 
(0.1%) under private self-help groups,.  
The distribution of non-agricultural establishments and employment therein by type of 
ownership with rural-urban break-up has been given in Table 4.5 and Table 4.6.

4.8.2 Social group of owners 
Majority of proprietary non-agricultural establishments were owned by social groups 
‘Others’ with 62.8% of total non-agricultural establishments having the employment share of 
69.94%. In urban areas, 68.25% establishments were under ownership of ‘others’, that 
employed 74.49% of the workers. The share of social groups ‘others’ in rural areas was 
53.45% employing 58.43% of the workers. 
In rural areas, majority of proprietary non-agricultural establishments were owned by 
OBCs (31.6%), where in 28.41% of the total non-agricultural establishments work force in 
rural areas were engaged.  In urban areas OBC owners were 19.41% employing 15.43% 
workers.  The OBC owners owned 23.9% of establishments at State level with an 
employment share of 19.11%. 
The SC owners owned 9.24% (9.43% in rural, 9.13% in urban) of the total non-
agricultural establishments in the State. In these establishments about 6.84 lakh persons 
7.74% (8.51% in rural, 7.44% in urban) were engaged. Similarly, ST owners owned 4.06% 
(5.51% in rural, 3.21% in urban) of the total non-agricultural establishments.  Wherein 2.83 
lakh persons about 3.21% (4.65% in rural, 2.64% in urban) were engaged.

At State level, Hindus owned 71.34% of the proprietary non-agricultural 
establishments, which employed 69.53% of the workforce.  This distribution was 78.5%

4.10 Premises Status 
It has been observed that about 26.89 lakh (59.16%) of the non-agricultural 
establishments operated from outside the household with fixed structure in the State. In rural 
areas, 52.11% and in urban areas 63.66% establishments were operating from outside 
household with a fixed structure. 
About 11.35 lakh (24.96%) non-agricultural establishments were found to be 
operating inside household. This percentage is higher (31.82%) for rural areas as compared to 
urban areas (20.58%). 
About 7.22 lakh (15.88%) non-agricultural establishments were operated from outside 
household without fixed structure. In rural area, 16.07 % establishments were operating from 
outside household without fixed structure, whereas in urban area this figure was 15.76%.

\n--- PDF PAGE 75 ---\nCHAPTER V 
HANDICRAFT / HANDLOOM ESTABLISHMENTS 
5.1 General 
Due to paucity of data on Handicraft/handloom sector, it was decided by Government 
of India to capture the information regarding Handicraft & Handloom establishments during 
Sixth Economic Census. The purpose of inclusion of this item in the Economic Census was to 
assess the number of handicraft and handloom establishments and persons employed therein. 
5.2  Definition of Handicraft/Handloom Activity 
 Handicraft/Handloom describes a craft or occupation requiring skilled use of hands. 
Handicrafts are items made by hand, often using simple tools. These items can be functional, 
artistic and/ or traditional in nature. Thus, to summarise, skill of worker and use of hand are 
two prerequisites for handicraft. It must be predominantly made by hand. It does not matter if 
some machinery is also used in the process. Handicrafts range from the very simple (diyas, 
kulhars) to the very complex (stone temples, enamel jewellery) work; certain products like 
baskets, brooms, pots, diyas, mats and chiks could range from very simple to quite complex, 
but are traditionally considered as handicrafts even in their simplest forms. At the same time, 
certain handmade articles are not usually categorized as handicrafts. Activities involving 
human labour without skill are usually not included. 
 Handicraft/Handloom establishments are those which involve predominant role of 
human skills and performed by hands using traditional knowledge and simple tools. 
Two broad categories for exclusion comprised of the following activities: 
• All food items like papads, pickles, etc. which may be handmade. 
• Items like bricks, bidis, agarbattis, match sticks, firecrackers, etc. which are made by 
hand but not distinguished by distinctive skills. 
For the purpose of Sixth Economic Census, the word handicraft includes handlooms, 
so all manner of hand weaving activities were considered under the same. 
 
 
 
 
Sixth Economic Census 2013, Maharashtra
69
\n--- PDF PAGE 76 ---\n5.3  Establishment 
During the census, information was collected from each of the manufacturing unit 
where hand as well as skill is being used in the manufacturing activity. It was observed that 
the total number of handicraft/handloom establishments in the State were 84,676. Of which, 
41.59% are located in rural areas. Out of total establishments 56,746 (67.02%) establishments 
are running without hired workers.  Moreover, 88.23% of these establishments were 
perennial, 10.53% were seasonal and rests 1.24% were casual in nature. 
The top 5 districts, in terms of number of handicraft/handloom establishments are 
Mumbai Suburban (13.34%), Mumbai (11.87%), Kolhapur(11.33), Thane (9.12%) and 
Solapur (5.43%). The details are given in Tables 5.1.

\n--- PDF PAGE 78 ---\n5.4 Employment 
As per Sixth EC, about 2.15 lakh persons were employed in handicraft and handloom 
sector, of which 28.65 % of the persons were employed in establishments located in rural 
areas and 71.35 % of the persons were employed in establishments located in urban areas.

of employment was in establishments without hired workers and 61.18% of employment was 
in establishments with at least one hired workers.  The top five districts as per employment in 
handicraft sector were Mumbai (20.98%), Mumbai Suburban (19.28%), Thane (10.68%), 
Kolhapur (9.26%) and Solapur (3.35%).

Sixth Economic Census 2013, Maharashtra
\n--- PDF PAGE 89 ---\nCHAPTER VI 
WOMEN ENTREPRENEURS 
6.1 Introduction 
Women’s equal access and control over economic and financial resources is critical 
for the achievement of gender equality and empowerment of women as well as equitable and 
sustainable economic growth and development. 
Although data on women labour force, literacy, health aspects etc., are being 
generated regularly though regular Census and Surveys but data on women entrepreneurial 
ship at national level is rather scanty.  Therefore, during Sixth Economic Census, questions 
related to women entrepreneurship in proprietary ownership were added.  It covers sex, 
religion, social group, economic activity, number of workers employed (hired and not hired) 
and source of finance. 
It was observed that out of total 61.37 lakh establishments in the State, 6.64 lakh 
establishments were run by women entrepreneurs which accounts 10.82%.  The number of 
workers engaged in women owned & run establishments were 10.98 lakh, which were 7.57% 
of the total number of workers engaged in the State under different economic activities. 
 
6.2 Establishments 
The total number of establishments owned by women entrepreneurs was 6,64,300.  Of 
which, 3,46,216 (52.12 %) were located in rural areas and the remaining 3,18,084 (47.88%) 
were located in urban areas.  In the State, about 5,80,337  establishments (87.36%) were 
operated without hired workers and 83,963 (12.64%) were operated with hired workers.  The 
percentage of establishments without hired workers in rural areas was 93.56%, whereas, it 
was 80.62% in urban areas.  The number of women establishments involved in agricultural 
activities was 1,78,422  constituting  26.86 % of the total number of establishments owned by 
women. 
The top five districts having highest percentage share in total number of women 
owned establishments in the State were Kolhapur (15.07%),Satara (8.19%), Pune (7.97%), 
Solapur (7.62%) and Mumbai Suburban (7.29 %).  The district wise total numbers of 
establishments under women entrepreneurship and type of establishments are depicted in 
Table 6.1.

10.98 lakh. Of which 5.03 lakh (45.85%) and 5.95 lakh (54.15%) persons were employed in

(64.13%) were working in establishments without hired workers whereas, 3.94 lakh (35.87%) 
persons were working in establishments with hired workers.  The top five districts by number 
of persons employed in women owned establishments, were Kolhapur 12.72% (1.40 lakh), 
Mumbai Suburban 11.25% (1.23 lakh), Pune 9.91% (1.09 lakh), Thane 8.47% (0.93 lakh) and  
Satara 6.93% (0.76 lakh).

\n--- PDF PAGE 98 ---\n6.4  Women entrepreneur by nature of operation  
It was observed that, about 92.28% of the women owned establishments were 
perennial, 6.72% were seasonal and remaining 1% was casual.  The nature of operation of 
workers engaged in these establishment ’s shows that, 93.29% of the workers work in 
perennial establishments, 5.90% work in seasonal establishments whereas remaining 0.81% 
works in casual establishments.  The percentage share of perennial establishments was 
94.35% in urban areas, whereas it was 90.39% in rural areas.

\n--- PDF PAGE 104 ---\nCHAPTER VII 
COMPARISON OF RESULTS OF ECONOMIC CENSUSES 
Fifth EC (2005) and Sixth EC (2013) 
7.1  Growth of establishments and employment 
 It is seen that the overall compound annual growth rate (CAGR) of establishments 
per year during 2005-2013 was 5%. Growth rate per annum in urban areas was 3.90%, 
whereas in rural areas it was 6.05%. The total growth in number of establishments during 
2005-13 was 47.79%. 
As far as employment is concerned, overall CAGR of 4.09% was observed during 
2005-13.  During 2005-13, a CAGR of 4.58% was observed in rural areas and a CAGR of 
3.75% was observed in urban areas. The total growth in number of workers during      
2005-2013 was 37.85%. 
The number of establishments and employment and inter-census growth rate of 
establishments & employment in respect of rural, urban and combined over the period 
2005-2013 is given in Table 7.1. 
Table  7.1  Number of establishments and employment for Fifth and Sixth EC's

(Fifth EC) & 2013 (Sixth EC).

Sixth ECs in number of establishments and number of workers respectively.  The growth in 
establishments was 59.93% in rural area and 35.85% in urban area. Similar trend can be 
seen in employment growth with 43.13% in rural area and 34.30% in urban area 
respectively. The district wise percentage growth in total number of establishments

\n--- PDF PAGE 108 ---\n7.3  Agricultural establishments 
It was observed that the overall CAGR of agricultural establishments increased to 
12.88% during 2005-13.  The increasing trend has been noticed both in rural (12.82%) and 
urban (14.08%) areas of the State.  The total growth in number of agricultural 
establishments during 2005-2013 was 163.53%. 
Similar trend of CAGR can be seen in employment in the agricultural 
establishments with a surge to 12.19% during 2005-13.  The total growth in employment in 
agricultural establishments during 2005-2013 was 151.06%. The details of the agricultural 
establishments, employment and growth are given in Table 7.4.

\n--- PDF PAGE 109 ---\n7.5  Average employment per establishment 
The average employment per establishment at State level has reduced from 2.68 in 
2005 to 2.36 in 2013. 
In case of agricultural establishments, average employment per establishment 
declined from 1.74 in 2005 to 1.66 in 2013. However, the average employment per 
establishment in non-agricultural establishments declined from2.83 in Fifth EC (2005) to 
2.61 in Sixth EC (2013). 
Average employment per establishment has shown a declining trend at State level 
and also both in rural & urban areas.

\n--- PDF PAGE 185 ---\nAnnexure IV 
CONCEPTS AND DEFINITIONS 
Concepts and definitions of some of the important terms used in the Sixth Economic 
Census are given below: 
Establishment 
The establishment is a unit situated in a single location in which predominantly one 
kind of economic activity is carried out such that at least a part of the goods and/or services 
produced by the unit goes for sale (i.e. entire produce is not for sole consumption). 
Agricultural Establishment 
An agricultural establishment for the purpose of Economic Census is one, which is 
engaged in production of agricultural goods (other than crop production & plantation by the 
farmers or a group of farmers or any agency), agricultural services, hunting, trapping & game 
propagation, where at least some part of the production or services is sold out. Establishments 
engaged in activities pertaining to crop production and plantations though in the agriculture 
sector are excluded from the coverage. Thus primarily cultivators themselves are excluded 
from the coverage of Sixth Economic Census. 
Non-Agricultural Establishment 
Establishments engaged in activities other than agricultural activities are termed as 
non-agricultural establishments. 
Household 
A household is a group of persons usually living together and taking their meals from 
a common kitchen. It includes temporary stay-away (those whose total period of absence 
from household is expected to be less than six months) but excludes temporary visitors and 
guests (with expected total stay of less than 6 months). A group of persons, who are unrelated 
to each other, live in a census house but do not take their meals from a common kitchen 
would not constitute an institutional household. 
Census House 
A census house is a building or a part of a building having a separate main entrance 
from the road or common courtyard or staircase, etc., used, or recognized as a separate unit. It 
may be occupied or vacant. It may be used for residential, commercial or for both purposes. 
 
Sixth Economic Census 2013, Maharashtra
181
\n--- PDF PAGE 186 ---\nEnumeration Block 
The ultimate area unit of enumeration in the Sixth EC is Population Census (2011) 
Enumeration Block (EB). An EB broadly covers about 120 households or a population of 
nearly 600 or so. 
Handicraft/Handloom Activity 
Handicraft describes a craft or occupation requiring skilled use of hands. Handicrafts 
are items made by hand, often using simple tools. These items can be functional, artistic and/ 
or traditional in nature. 
Nature of Operation 
If the entrepreneurial activity is carried on or likely to be carried on throughout the 
year more or less regularly, it is treated as perennial activity. If the activity of the 
establishment is confined to a particular season i.e. fixed months of a year, the same is called 
the seasonal activity. The economic activity of the establishment which is neither perennial 
nor seasonal is termed as casual. 
Worker (Person Employed) 
All persons (including children under 15 years of age) working on the last working 
day prior to the date of fieldwork in the establishment, either as owners, members of the 
household, co-owner or partner or family members helping the owner in running the 
establishment including other persons engaged by the establishment, whether hired or not, 
besides regular and salaried employees, casual/daily wage labourers are considered as 
workers for the establishment. 
 
Concepts and Definitions
